# October 7 attacks

This article is about the 2023 attacks on Israel from Gaza. For the war initiated by the attack, see [Gaza war](74998519.md). For the October 7, 2001, United States attack on Afghanistan, see [United States invasion of Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_invasion_of_Afghanistan).

On October 7, 2023, a series of coordinated armed incursions from the [blockaded](19224310.md) [Gaza Strip](12047.md) into the [Gaza envelope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_envelope) of [southern Israel](844338.md) were carried out by [Hamas](13913.md) and several other [Palestinian militant groups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_militant_groups) during the Jewish holiday of [Simchat Torah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simchat_Torah). In response, Israel launched [a major military operation](74998519.md) in Gaza.

The attacks began with a [barrage of at least 4,300 rockets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Palestinian_rocket_attacks_on_Israel_in_2023) launched into Israel[^cite_note-\:18-34][^cite_note-\:19-35][^cite_note-\:16-36] and vehicle-transported and [powered paraglider](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Powered_paraglider) incursions into Israel.[^cite_note-Kubovich_Day_1-37][^cite_note-scmp8oct-38] Hamas militants breached the [Gaza–Israel barrier](478337.md), attacking military bases and massacring civilians in 21 communities, including [Be'eri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Be'eri_massacre), [Kfar Aza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kfar_Aza_massacre), [Nir Oz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nir_Oz_massacre), [Netiv Haasara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netiv_HaAsara_massacre), and [Alumim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alumim_massacre). According to an [Israel Defense Forces](39237.md) (IDF) report that revised the estimate on the number of attackers, 6,000 Gazans breached the border in 119 locations into Israel, including 3,800 from the elite [Nukhba forces](75044554.md) and 2,200 civilians and other militants.[^cite_note-\:15-39][^cite_note-\:18-34][^cite_note-\:19-35] Additionally, the IDF report estimated 1,000 Gazans fired rockets from the Gaza Strip, bringing the total number of participants on Hamas's side to 7,000.[^cite_note-\:15-39][^cite_note-\:18-34][^cite_note-\:19-35]

In total, at least 1,195 people were killed by the attacks:[^cite_note-hrw20240717-24][^cite_note-44][^cite_note-45] at least 828 civilians[^cite_note-hrw20240717-24][^cite_note-timesofisrael20240806-25] (including 36 children[^cite_note-social_security_data-26] and 71 foreign nationals)[^cite_note-social_security_data-26] and at least 367 members of the [security forces](315904.md). 364 civilians were killed while they were attending the [Nova music festival](75007485.md) and many more wounded.[^cite_note-toi-46][^cite_note-Israel_revises_Hamas_attack_death_toll_to_around_1,200-47] 251 Israeli and non-Israeli civilians and soldiers were [taken as hostages to the Gaza Strip](75025451.md).[^cite_note-\:16-36][^cite_note-jerusalempost19oct-48][^cite_note-\:23-49][^cite_note-guardian7oct-50][^cite_note-51] Numerous sources have reported dozens of cases of [rape and sexual assault](75436869.md), but Hamas officials denied the involvement of their fighters.[^cite_note-52] At least 14 Israeli civilians were killed by the IDF's use of the [Hannibal Directive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hannibal_Directive).[^cite_note-\:63-53][^cite_note-\:20-54]

Hamas said its attack was in response to the continued [Israeli occupation](2781576.md), the [blockade of the Gaza Strip](19224310.md), the expansion of [illegal Israeli settlements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_law_and_Israeli_settlements), rising [Israeli settler violence](47246246.md), recent escalations[^cite_note-aj7oct-invasion-55][^cite_note-McKernan-2023-56] and Israel's holding of Palestinian prisoners,[^cite_note-aj9-57][^cite_note-Pacchiani2023-58] and that it was in defense of the Palestinian people and their cause.[^cite_note-Davis-59] Experts disagree on whether Hamas sought to induce the destruction of Israel, or to establish a Palestinian state with [1967 borders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Line_\(Israel\)). The day was labelled the bloodiest in [Israel's history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Israel_\(1948%E2%80%93present\)) and "the deadliest for Jews since [the Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Holocaust)" by many figures and media outlets in [the West](21208200.md), including then-US president [Joe Biden](145422.md).[^cite_note-\:2-60][^cite_note-\:14-61] Some have [alleged that the attack was an act of genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allegations_of_genocide_in_the_October_7_attacks) or a [genocidal massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genocidal_massacre) against Israelis.[^cite_note-\:9-62][^cite_note-\:10-63][^cite_note-\:11-64] The governments of 44 countries denounced the attack and described it as [terrorism](2646043.md), while some Arab and Muslim-majority countries blamed Israel's occupation of the [Palestinian territories](241405.md) as the root cause of the attack.[^cite_note-WI-Waldo-65][^cite_note-Guardian-Michaelson-66][^cite_note-auto2-67]

## Names

Hamas and other Palestinian armed groups codenamed the attacks **Operation Al-Aqsa Flood**,[^cite_note-68][^cite_note-UN_Fact_Sheet-1] while in Israel they are referred to as **Black Saturday**[^cite_note-69][^cite_note-70] or the **Simchat Torah Massacre**.[^cite_note-71][^cite_note-72] Internationally and commonly in Israel,[^cite_note-73] the attacks are called the **October 7 attacks**.[^cite_note-SAA-74][^cite_note-sky1011-75][^cite_note-G0911-76]

## Background

Further information: [Background to the Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Background_to_the_Gaza_war) and [Israeli–Palestinian conflict](46216.md)



The [Gaza Strip](12047.md) under [Israeli blockade](19224310.md), with Israeli/Egyptian-controlled borders and limited fishing zone in September 2023

### Israeli occupation and blockade of the Gaza Strip

Further information: [Blockade of the Gaza Strip](19224310.md) and [Israeli-Gaza conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli-Gaza_conflict)

According to [international humanitarian law](1093604.md) scholars, [Palestinians](23267.md) in the [Gaza Strip](12047.md) has been subjected to some form of [belligerent](816214.md) [Israeli occupation](2781576.md) since 1967.[^cite_note-\:24-77]

The Gaza Strip—a coastal strip 28 miles long and 6 miles wide with small towns and [refugee camps](400347.md) delineated by the [Green Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Line_\(Israel\)) of the [1949 Armistice Agreements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1949_Armistice_Agreements)—was constituted in the aftermath of the [Nakba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nakba) and the [1948 Palestine war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1948_Palestine_war), when it came under [Egyptian administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Arab_Republic_occupation_of_the_Gaza_Strip) (1949–67).[^cite_note-\:24-77] In the [1967 Arab–Israeli War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1967_Arab-Israeli_War) ([Six-Day War](29328.md)), the Israeli military [occupied the Gaza Strip](76465951.md) (1967–2005), imposing restrictions on movement and goods into the territory since the early 1990s.[^cite_note-\:24-77][^cite_note-78][^cite_note-79]

In 2005, [Israel disengaged](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_disengagement_from_the_Gaza_Strip)[^cite_note-80] from the Gaza Strip by withdrawing its forces and dismantling and evacuating its 21 [settlements](15123.md) there, though it redeployed and maintained troops on the borders outside of Gaza and took exclusive control of the airspace over Gaza and its territorial waters.[^cite_note-\:24-77][^cite_note-britannica-81] Israel [maintained restrictions and active control on the movement of people and goods](19224310.md) in the Gaza Strip, with the stated aims of preventing the smuggling of weapons into Gaza and exerting economic pressure on Hamas.[^cite_note-\:110-82] In the view of the majority of the relevant scholarship, the [United Nations](31769.md),[^cite_note-83] the [International Court of Justice](72240449.md), and many other international non-governmental humanitarian and legal organizations, Israel has continued to be regarded as an [occupying power](816214.md) in the Gaza Strip, which has continued to be regarded as among the [occupied Palestinian territories](2781576.md).[^cite_note-occ-84][^cite_note-\:21-85] The official Israeli view and certain pro-Israel scholars consider the Gaza Strip to be unoccupied.[^cite_note-occ-84][^cite_note-\:21-85]

### Hamas

Further information: [Gaza Strip under Hamas](38626632.md)

The Islamic Resistance Movement ([Hamas](13913.md)), is a [Palestinian nationalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_nationalist)[^cite_note-natdem-86] [Islamist](15012.md) movement that formed in 1987. It is the largest Islamist movement in the Palestinian territories.[^cite_note-auto12-87] They maintain an uncompromising stance on the "complete liberation of Palestine", often using [political violence](2646043.md) to achieve their goals.[^cite_note-auto12-87] Hamas has issued [contradictory statements](13913.md#Policies_towards_Israel_and_Palestine) concerning the acceptability of the 1967 borders. Hamas has been responsible for numerous suicide bombings[^cite_note-88][^cite_note-89] and rocket attacks targeting Israeli civilians.[^cite_note-90] Australia, Canada, the EU, Japan, New Zealand, and the UK have designated Hamas a "terrorist organisation". In 2010 it attempted to derail the peace talks between Israel and the PA. In 2017, it adopted a [new charter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Hamas_charter), removing [antisemitic](1078.md) language and shifting focus from Jews to Zionists.[^cite_note-91] Scholars differ on Hamas's objectives, with some saying it seeks a Palestinian state within 1967 borders[^cite_note-borders19672-92] while others believe Hamas still [seeks the destruction of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calls_for_the_destruction_of_Israel).[^cite_note-93][^cite_note-94]

### International warnings

Before the attack, Saudi Arabia warned Israel of an "explosion" as a result of the continued occupation.[^cite_note-edwardwong-95] Egypt said it warned Israel days before the attack that "an explosion of the situation \[was] coming, and very soon, and it would be big."[^cite_note-egyptint-96] Israel denied receiving such a warning,[^cite_note-97] although [Michael McCaul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_McCaul), Chairman of the [US House Foreign Relations Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_House_Foreign_Relations_Committee), said that warnings were given three days before the attack.[^cite_note-auto20-98] According to *[Haaretz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haaretz)*, Israeli prime minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) was warned of a Hamas plot to attack Israel in a September 2023 phone call with UAE president [Mohamed bin Zayed Al Nahyan](2121945.md). The UAE had been told of the attack by an intermediary, who warned that Hamas leader [Yahya Sinwar](53195047.md) was plotting a "huge surprise". The UAE did not comment on the *Haaretz* report, although Netanyahu denied it and told his lawyers to sue the newspaper and the report's authors, [Shlomi Eldar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shlomi_Eldar) and [Ruth Yuval](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruth_Yaval?action=edit\&redlink=1).[^cite_note-99][^cite_note-100]

### Events leading to the attack

See also: [Timeline of the Israeli–Palestinian conflict in 2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_the_Israeli%E2%80%93Palestinian_conflict_in_2023)

Throughout 2023, increased settler attacks displaced hundreds of Palestinians.[^cite_note-apn1-101]  In April, [clashes occurred around the Al-Aqsa Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Al-Aqsa_clashes), a contested holy site in Jerusalem. In May, [clashes occurred](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/May_2023_Gaza%E2%80%93Israel_clashes) between Israel and the [Palestinian Islamic Jihad](13093253.md) group.[^cite_note-apn1-101]

Tensions between Israel and Hamas rose in September 2023, and *[The Washington Post](102226.md)* wrote that the two were "on the brink of war".[^cite_note-wapo-102] On September 13, five Palestinians were killed at the border.[^cite_note-104] Israel said it found explosives hidden in a shipment and halted all exports from Gaza;[^cite_note-wapo-102] Hamas denied Israel's claims.[^cite_note-exports-105] [Reuters](18998750.md) quoted Palestinians who said that the several-day ban affected thousands of families.[^cite_note-exports-105] In response to the ban, Hamas put its forces on high alert and conducted military exercises with other groups, including openly practicing storming Israeli settlements.[^cite_note-wapo-102] Hamas also allowed Palestinians to resume protests at the [Gaza–Israel barrier](478337.md).[^cite_note-wapo-102] On September 29, [Qatar](8238258.md), the UN, and Egypt mediated an agreement between Israeli and Hamas officials in the Gaza Strip to reopen closed crossing points and deescalate tensions;[^cite_note-106] the total number of Gazans with work permits in Israel stood at 17,000.[^cite_note-107]

### Operational planning

For two years, Hamas used [hardwired phone lines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Landline) within Gaza's tunnel network, nicknamed the "[Gaza metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_metro)", to covertly communicate, evade Israeli intelligence, and plan Operation Al-Aqsa Flood.[^cite_note-108][^cite_note-109]

In the months preceding the attack, Hamas publicly released videos of its militants preparing to attack Israel. A video released in December 2022 showed Hamas training to take hostages, while another video showed Hamas practicing [paragliding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paragliding).[^cite_note-110] On September 12, Hamas posted a video of its fighters training to blast through the border.[^cite_note-111] After the attack, the IDF said that Hamas had extensively studied the military bases and communities near the border.[^cite_note-\:0-112][^cite_note-113]

*[The Wall Street Journal](173070.md)* has accused Iran of being behind the attack.[^cite_note-114] U.S. officials[^cite_note-Training-115] and Iran have denied this.[^cite_note-116]

According to a [New York Times](30680.md) investigation, a memo dated August 24, 2022, and apparently written by [Yahya Sinwar](53195047.md) described a Hamas attack on Israel similar to October 7 attacks. It called for bulldozers to breach the Gaza-Israel fence and for multiple assault waves. It urged "Stomp on the heads of soldiers" and listed "opening fire on soldiers at point-blank range, slaughtering some of them with knives, blowing up tanks". It ordered entry into residential areas to set them on fire "with gasoline or diesel from a tanker", preparing "two or three operations" in which "an entire neighborhood, kibbutz, or something similar will be burned". It directed unit commanders to film and broadcast the acts to mobilize Palestinians in the West Bank, Arabs in Israel, and "our nation" to "join the revolution".[^cite_note-\:1-117]

The IDF reportedly seized over 10,000 weapons following the attack. The arsenal included RPGs, mines, sniper rifles, drones, [thermobaric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thermobaric) rockets, and other advanced weapons. According to Israeli sources, documents and maps seized from Hamas militants indicated that Hamas intended a coordinated, month-long operation to invade and occupy Israeli towns, cities, and kibbutzim, including attacking [Ashkelon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashkelon) by sea and reaching [Kiryat Gat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiryat_Gat), 20 miles (32 km) into Israel. The scale of weapons, supplies, and plans indicated, according to Israel, that Hamas intended to inflict mass casualties on Israeli civilians and military forces over an extended period.[^cite_note-118] Western and Middle Eastern security officials gathered evidence suggesting that Hamas intended to invade as far as the West Bank, had the initial attack been more successful.[^cite_note-119]

### Israeli intelligence failure

According to *[The New York Times](30680.md)*, Israeli officials had obtained detailed attack plans more than a year before the attack. The document described operational plans and targets, including the size and location of Israeli forces, and raised questions in Israel about how Hamas learned these details. The document provided a plan that included a large-scale rocket assault before an invasion, drones to knock out the surveillance cameras and automated guns that Israel has stationed along the border, and gunmen invading Israel, including with paragliders. *[The Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Times)* reported, "Hamas followed the blueprint with shocking precision." According to *The Times*, the document was widely circulated among Israeli military and intelligence leadership, who largely dismissed the plan as beyond Hamas's capabilities, though it was unclear whether the political leadership was informed. In July 2023, a member of the [Israeli signals intelligence unit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unit_8200) alerted her superiors that Hamas was conducting preparations for the assault, saying, "I utterly refute that the scenario is imaginary". An Israeli colonel ignored her concerns.[^cite_note-120]

The official investigation by Israel's domestic intelligence agency, [Shin Bet](311752.md), found that the agency failed to provide the warning that could have prevented the massacre. The head of Shin Bet convened a situation assessment in the early hours of October 7 to discuss the intelligence received from Gaza; in the end, only a low-level warning was issued, and a small team specialising in thwarting limited attacks was sent to the south. According to the report the counterintelligence service of Hamas had been highly effective in preventing the gathering of intelligence in Gaza.[^cite_note-shabak-121]

According to the findings of the investigation, Shin Bet warned the Prime Minister that Hamas was not deterred and objected to the prevailing divide-and-rule policy of the Israeli government.[^cite_note-shabak-121]

According to *[Haaretz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haaretz)*, [Shin Bet](311752.md) and IDF military commanders discussed a possible threat to the Nova music festival near kibbutz [Re'im](17756866.md) just hours before the attack, but the festival's organizers were not warned.[^cite_note-122][^cite_note-123]

As revealed by a [BBC](19344654.md) investigation, surveillance reports suggested that Hamas was planning a significant operation against Israel, but senior IDF officers repeatedly ignored the warnings.[^cite_note-Cuddy-124]

A *Haaretz* investigation found that incompetence in the IDF's higher ranks, including refusal to acknowledge Hamas's preparations for the attacks, was a major contributor to the severity of the October attacks. The IDF had reduced funding and manpower dedicated to observing Hamas, focusing primarily on rocket sites and ignoring Hamas training and troop movements, and the activities of the Hamas military leadership. Exercises in which Hamas attacks were simulated found the Gaza division's response lacking. [Cultural conformity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_conformity) was fostered among officers and dissent discouraged. Senior officers often silenced subordinates to maintain their positions, contributing to a [toxic atmosphere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toxic_workplace) in which lower-ranking officers were apprehensive about questioning the decisions of their superiors - and tended to refrain from doing so.[^cite_note-125]

The Israeli defense establishment had warned the Prime Minister that political upheavals related to the significant judicial overhauls had signaled to adversaries that the country was vulnerable, although that very apparatus had ignored its own detection of Hamas's "Walls of Jericho" mass-invasion plan a year prior to the event, as well as, the detection of border movements in the hours leading up to the invasion.[^cite_note-126]

## Attacks

For a more comprehensive list, see [List of military engagements during the Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_military_engagements_during_the_Gaza_war).

At around 6\:30 a.m. [Israel Summer Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Summer_Time) (UTC+3) on Saturday, October 7, 2023, Hamas announced the start of Operation Al-Aqsa Flood.[^cite_note-aj1-127] [Al-Qassam Brigades](1703333.md) commander [Mohammed Deif](92534.md) gave a speech mentioning Israel's [16-year blockade of Gaza](19224310.md), Israeli incursions in West Bank cities, violence at Al-Aqsa mosque, [Israeli settler violence](47246246.md) with the army's support, the confiscation of property and demolition of homes, arbitrarily detaining Palestinians for years until "they wither from cancer and disease", Israel's neglect for international law, American-Western support, and international silence. He then described the operation, that they had drawn the line, and that it was to end "the last occupation on Earth".[^cite_note-128][^cite_note-aj1-127] Shortly thereafter, [Ismail Haniyeh](41207463.md) made a similar announcement in a televised address.[^cite_note-129]

### Participating and supporting organizations

In addition to Hamas, several Palestinian militant groups voiced support for the operation and participated in it to some extent. The [National Resistance Brigades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Resistance_Brigades), the armed wing of the [Marxist–Leninist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marxist%E2%80%93Leninist) [Democratic Front for the Liberation of Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Front_for_the_Liberation_of_Palestine) (DFLP), confirmed their participation in the operation through their military spokesman [Abu Khaled](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Khaled_\(DFLP\)),[^cite_note-130] saying it had lost three fighters in combat with the IDF.[^cite_note-DFLP-6] The [PFLP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PFLP) (a Palestinian Marxist–Leninist / secular nationalist political party)[^cite_note-pflp-5] and the [Lions' Den](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lions'_Den_\(militant_group\)) group (a nonpartisan militant group based in the [West Bank](33209.md)) voiced support for the operation and declared maximum alertness and general mobilization among their troops. Nine individuals employed by [UNRWA](32270.md) were accused by Israel of participation, and after a months-long internal investigation, were fired for possible involvement.[^cite_note-131]

### Rocket fire

For a more comprehensive list, see [List of Palestinian rocket attacks on Israel in 2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Palestinian_rocket_attacks_on_Israel_in_2023).



Aftermath of Hamas rocket hit on the maternity ward of [Barzilai Medical Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barzilai_Medical_Center), a hospital in southern Israel, during the Hamas-led attack on Israel

[^cite_note-132]

Deif said more than 5,000 rockets had been fired from the Gaza Strip into Israel in a span of 20 minutes at the start of the operation. Israeli sources reported the launch of 4,300 projectiles from Gaza,[^cite_note-\:18-34][^cite_note-\:19-35] killing twelve and injuring dozens, most of them from the Bedouin community.[^cite_note-scmp8oct-38][^cite_note-aj7oct-133][^cite_note-guardian7oct-50] Explosions were reported in areas surrounding Gaza and in the [Sharon Plain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sharon_Plain), including [Gedera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gedera), [Herzliya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herzliya), [Beersheba](312755.md), [Tel Aviv](31453.md), and [Ashkelon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashkelon).[^cite_note-ynet-134][^cite_note-bbc1-135] Air raid sirens were activated in [Beersheba](312755.md), [Jerusalem](16043.md), [Rehovot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rehovot), [Rishon LeZion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rishon_LeZion), and [Palmachim Airbase](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palmachim_Airbase).[^cite_note-f24-1-136][^cite_note-cnn1-137] Hamas issued a call to arms, with Deif calling on "[Muslims](19541.md) everywhere to launch an attack".[^cite_note-guardian7oct-50] In the evening Hamas launched another barrage of about 150 rockets towards Israel, with explosions reported in [Yavne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yavne), [Givatayim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Givatayim), [Bat Yam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bat_Yam), [Beit Dagan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_Dagan), [Tel Aviv](31453.md), and [Rishon LeZion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rishon_LeZion).[^cite_note-aj7oct-133]

### Incursions into southern Israel





Approximate situation on October 7–8

Hamas militants firing into a moving civilian car in [Sderot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sderot)

Militants killing Israeli civilians in kibbutz [Mefalsim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mefalsim)

Simultaneously, around 6,000 Palestinian militants and civilians infiltrated Israel from Gaza breaching the border in 119 places.[^cite_note-138][^cite_note-\:18-34][^cite_note-\:19-35] The infiltration was executed using trucks, [pickup trucks](47877.md), motorcycles, bulldozers, speedboats, and [powered paragliders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Powered_paragliding).[^cite_note-ap1-139][^cite_note-aj1-127][^cite_note-nyt7oct-140]

In a raid on the Sderot police station, 30 Israeli policemen and civilians were killed.[^cite_note-Margulies-141] Early in the attack the computer systems were destroyed, disabling communication and delaying the response to the attacks.[^cite_note-Dr._Levi_West_-_tactics_to_strategy/-142][^cite_note-iaudio_Dr._Levi_West_-_Modern_War_Institute-143][^cite_note-Levi_West_-_Irregular_Warfare_Podcast-144]

Images and videos on social media showed heavily armed and masked militants dressed in black fatigues riding pickup trucks[^cite_note-bbc1-135][^cite_note-cnn1-137] and opening fire in Sderot, killing dozens of Israeli civilians and soldiers and setting homes on fire.[^cite_note-f24-2-145] Other videos appeared to show Israelis taken prisoner, a burning Israeli tank,[^cite_note-AP-146][^cite_note-guardian7oct-50] and militants driving Israeli military vehicles.[^cite_note-bbc1-135] Israeli first responders recovered documents from militants' bodies with instructions to attack civilians, including elementary schools and a youth center, to "kill as many people as possible", and to take hostages for use in future negotiations.[^cite_note-147][^cite_note-148][^cite_note-149] The UN's [Pramila Patten](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pramila_Patten) and [Commission of Inquiry](69971286.md) concluded in their reports that the authenticity of these alleged instruction documents, claimed to have been retrieved from Hamas militants, could not be substantiated.[^cite_note-\:13-150][^cite_note-OHCHR3-151] Some of the militants wore [body cameras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Body_camera) to record the acts, presumably for propaganda purposes.[^cite_note-152] In recordings of Hamas commanders during the attack, published by The New York Times, one commander is heard saying: "Document the scenes of horror, now, and broadcast them on TV channels to the whole world", and "Slaughter them. End the children of Israel".[^cite_note-\:1-117]

The morning of the attack, an Israeli military spokesman said that the militants from Gaza had entered Israel through at least seven locations[^cite_note-ap1-139] and invaded four small rural Israeli communities, the border city of Sderot, and two military bases from both land and sea.[^cite_note-nyt7oct-140] Israeli media reported that seven communities came under Hamas control, including [Nahal Oz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nahal_Oz), [Kfar Aza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kfar_Aza), [Magen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magen,_Israel), [Be'eri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Be'eri), and [Sufa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sufa,_Israel).[^cite_note-guardianlive-153] The [Erez Crossing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erez_Crossing) was reported to have come under Hamas control, enabling militants to enter Israel from Gaza.[^cite_note-aj7oct-133] Israeli Police Commissioner [Kobi Shabtai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kobi_Shabtai) said there were 21 active high-confrontation locations in southern Israel.[^cite_note-jordan1-154]

*The New York Times* reported that an Israeli intelligence document prepared weeks after the attack found that Hamas had breached the border fence in over 30 separate locations.[^cite_note-nyt-2023-military-gbmoctf-155]

Starting at 6.30 a.m. the same day, [a massacre unfolded at an outdoor music festival](75007485.md) near [Re'im](17756866.md), resulting in at least 360 dead and many others missing. Witnesses recounted militants on motorcycles opening fire on fleeing participants, who were already dispersing due to rocket fire that had wounded some attendees; some were also taken hostage.[^cite_note-Testimony-fr-156][^cite_note-npr-157][^cite_note-158] Militants killed civilians at [Nir Oz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nir_Oz_massacre),[^cite_note-cnn1-137][^cite_note-159] Be'eri, and [Netiv HaAsara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netiv_HaAsara_massacre), where they took hostages[^cite_note-160][^cite_note-161] and set fire to homes,[^cite_note-Psyduck_(he)-162] as well as in [kibbutzim](106200.md) around the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-Psyduck_(he)-162] Around 50 civilians were killed in the [Kfar Aza massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kfar_Aza_massacre), 108 in the [Be'eri massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Be'eri_massacre), and 15 people in the [Netiv HaAsara massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netiv_HaAsara_attack).[^cite_note-163][^cite_note-164] Militants killed 16 or 17 Thai and Nepalese employees during the [Kibbutz Alumim massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alumim_massacre).[^cite_note-165][^cite_note-166]

Other militants carried out an amphibious [landing in Zikim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Zikim).[^cite_note-bbc1-135][^cite_note-167] Palestinian sources claim that the local Israeli army base was stormed.[^cite_note-168] The IDF said it had killed two attackers on the beach and destroyed four vessels, including two rubber boats. Militants also [attacked a military base outside Nahal Oz](75068288.md), leaving 66 soldiers dead[^cite_note-169] and taking seven hostage.[^cite_note-170][^cite_note-\:12-171] An IDF [fire investigation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fire_investigation) found that the militants had "ignited substances... that contain toxic gasses which can cause suffocation within minutes, or even less" both at the base and in civilian locations.[^cite_note-\:12-171]
